| `emailOutbox(settings)`, `EmailOutbox`, `OutboxSettings`, `ComposeEmail`, `OutboxLogEntry` (`outbox.ts`) | `sendDue(stopping?, to?)` sends every email due by the clock given (with `to`, only those to these recipients), the one due longest first: each is taken with a row lock (`FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`), so another outbox skips it, written by `compose` in that transaction and handed to `sendEmail`; then recorded sent, or with the attempt and what failed (its kind and code, e.g. `send: EENVELOPE 550`, never the message) and logged; an email cut off by a stop is sent again at the next start (at least once). Once the signal given to `sendDue` is aborted, it ends after the email in progress. Rejects only when the database cannot be reached. |
| `runEvery(waitMs, task, onError)`, `Running` (`run-every.ts`) | Runs a task at once and again each `waitMs` after the last run ended, never two at once; a failed run goes to `onError` and the next still comes; `stop()` aborts the signal each run is given, so it can end early, and resolves once the run in progress has ended. |
| `renderEmail(parts)`, `EmailParts`, `EmailBlock`, `TallyBar`, `Line`, `Inline`, `lineText(line)` (`render-email.ts`) | An email in the shared frame (grey page, white card 480 px wide, qando's symbol from `/email/logo-<hash>.png` of the site, named after its content so a new image gets a new address no mail client has a copy of, "Hola, {name}:" or "Hola:", a quiet footer under a thin rule), its body given as blocks (paragraph, lead, pills, quote, link, button, code; dates in full in green boxes, their connecting words smaller; a tally, the results page's legend and bars with more bars under a heading; a quiet underlined link) with bold pieces: both the HTML and the plain text are rendered from them, every value escaped in the HTML; the preview text is hidden at the top. A lead keeps close to the block it introduces, and joins a link or a code on one line in the plain text; a quiet link keeps close to the block above it. `lineText` writes a sentence in plain text. |
| `composeEventEmail(siteUrl)` (`event-emails.ts`) | The outbox's `compose` for the API: writes an event's email from the event as it is when sent, read in the sending transaction (an event changed after its email was queued is written as it now is, and "voting closed" counts every vote stored by then), its pages linked under `siteUrl`; the summary through `summaryEmail`, "voting closed" through `votingClosedEmail`. |
| `summaryEmail(data)`, `SummaryEmailData` (`summary-email.ts`) | The summary an organizer receives once the event is created: subject "Resumen de tu evento: {title}", its options as the results page's short labels (at most 8 pills: from 9, the first 7 and "+ N"), "Hora de {city}", the note quoted, the participation link to share, the last day to vote, and "Gestionar evento" to `/manage/:id`, without the token, which is never stored. |
| `votingClosedEmail(data)`, `VotingClosedEmailData` (`voting-closed-email.ts`) | "Votación cerrada", from the event's options and votes, its results computed as the results page's (`resultsOf`): subject "Votación cerrada: {title}", a preview that says the result, how many voted, the page's sentence for the outcome with up to three most voted dates in full ("Hora de {city}" when one has a time), the final count as the page's bars with the alternatives, "Confirmar fecha" to `/manage/:id` and "Ver los resultados completos"; with nobody voting, or nobody able to attend, "Gestionar evento" to extend the deadline or confirm a date anyway. A date is never called chosen: none is confirmed yet. |
| `shortOptionLabel(option)`, `longOptionLabel(option)`, `dayLabel(date)`, `zoneCity(timeZone)` (`date-labels.ts`) | Dates as the emails write them, in Spanish: an option as the results page's chart names it ("Sáb 11 Jul · 21:00", "Sáb 18 → Dom 19 Jul") and as its winning dates do, dates and times in bold ("**sábado 11** de **julio**, a las **21:00**"), a day in full as a sentence says it ("miércoles 1 de julio de 2026"), and the city that names a time zone ("New York"). |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/web/public/email` (the logo the emails show, over HTTP), `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`, `apps/api/src/modules/auth`, `apps/api/src/modules/events`.

### `apps/api/src/http`

**Summary:** Express pieces every module shares.

**Purpose:** Reading requests against the contract, answering failures, limiting requests, reading the session, keeping other sites' pages out, keeping answers out of caches, and the plumbing around them.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                        | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readRequest(endpoint, request)` (`read-request.ts`)                                          | Parses a request's path, headers and body with the endpoint's schemas, those it declares (an endpoint without path parameters or headers reads them as empty). Problems name where each broken part travels and its path, never its value (it may be a token or personal data).                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `contractBroken(problems)` (`read-request.ts`)                                                | The `400 VALIDATION_FAILED` body every router answers to a request that breaks the contract: each broken part, never its value.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `routePath(endpoint)` (`route-path.ts`)                                                       | The Express route of a contract path: `/api/events/{id}` → `/api/events/:id`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `errorBoundary(log)` (`error-boundary.ts`)                                                    | The last middleware: a request Express could not read is a `400`, any other failure a `500` in the shared error body, logged without anything that could carry a link, a token or personal data.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ErrorLog`, `ErrorLogEntry`, `logToStandardError` (`error-boundary.ts`)                       | What a failure log holds, and the log that writes it as one JSON line on standard error.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `describeError(error)` (`error-boundary.ts`)                                                  | What of an error may be logged: its name, its cause's name and code, its stack frames; never its message, which may quote a query's parameters.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `requestLimit(limit)`, `RequestLimit` (`rate-limit.ts`)                                       | Refuses the requests past `limit` in `windowMs` from one client address (IPv6 per /56 network) with `429 RATE_LIMITED` and `Retry-After`, before they reach the route (no database work). The count lives in memory: the API runs as one instance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `answerRateLimited(response, retryAfterSeconds)` (`rate-limit.ts`)                            | The `429 RATE_LIMITED` answer, never cached, with `Retry-After`; for limits a route applies itself (e.g. `sendQuota`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `rollingHourLimit(perHour)` (`rate-limit.ts`)                                                 | Refuses the requests of one client address past `perHour` in any hour with `429 RATE_LIMITED` and `Retry-After`, before they reach the route. The hour slides (`sendQuota`, on the system's clock like every limit per address): each request stops counting when it is an hour old, and a refused one counts nothing; IPv6 per /56 network.                                                                                                                        |
| `sendQuota(settings, now)`, `SendQuota`, `SendQuotaSettings`, `QuotaAnswer` (`send-quota.ts`) | Sends per key (an email, a client address) counted by the sends themselves, on the clock given: `take(key)` grants one, holding its place for an hour, or answers the seconds to wait (`gapMs` since the last one, `perHour` in any hour); `release()` gives back only that send's place (a delivery refused even after a later code went out frees nothing else). A refused ask takes nothing. In memory; keys without sends in the last hour are forgotten.       |
| `capabilityRateLimit()` (`rate-limit.ts`)                                                     | `requestLimit` of 60 requests a minute per client address over every capability endpoint together (voting, results and management), as long as the app mounts one instance on all of them.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `sessionOriginCheck(sessions)` (`session.ts`)                                                 | Refuses a change (any method but `GET`, `HEAD`, `OPTIONS`) carrying the session cookie from a page of another site, or without `Origin`, with `403 FORBIDDEN`, before the session is read (CSRF); a request without the cookie passes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `sessionReader(sessions)` (`session.ts`)                                                      | `sessionOriginCheck`, then signs each request in as the account of its session cookie and sends back the cookie renewed while reading it; a request without the cookie reads nothing. Mounted as a list of two handlers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `ownPagesOnly(sessions)` (`session.ts`)                                                       | Refuses any request from a page of another site, or without `Origin`, with `403 FORBIDDEN`, session cookie or not. For the routes that sign a browser in, so another site can neither sign the person into an account of its own (login CSRF) nor have codes sent from their browser, and for the ones only qando's pages have a use for (reading a date phrase), so no other site spends them from a visitor's address.                                            |
| `signedInAccount(request)` (`session.ts`)                                                     | The ID of the account the request is signed in as, once `sessionReader` read it; undefined otherwise.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `signedInOnly(sessions)` (`session.ts`) | For the routes only a session opens: `sessionReader`, then `401 UNAUTHENTICATED` to a request without a session, or with one that ended, before its body is read. Mounted as a list of handlers. |
| `signedInAccountId(request)` (`session.ts`) | The ID of the account of a request `signedInOnly` let through; on a route without it, it throws rather than let the request act as nobody. |
| `Sessions`, `SessionRead`, `SessionAccount` (`session.ts`)                                    | The port to the sessions: `carried(headers)` (a session cookie at all, no database work), `trusts(origin)` (a page of qando), `read(headers)` (a `SessionRead`: its `account`, a `SessionAccount` `{ id, name, email }` whose name is empty until the account has one, undefined when the session ended; and the renewed cookies to send back), `end(headers)` (deletes the session, then answers the cookies that clear it; rejects when it could not be deleted). |
| `noStore` (`no-store.ts`)                                                                     | Marks every answer of a route `Cache-Control: no-store`, also the refusals of the middleware mounted after it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `trustedProxies` (`trusted-proxies.ts`)                                                       | The proxy ranges between a client and the API, so `request.ip` is the client's real address.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `testClock(realNow)`, `TestClock` (`test-clock.ts`)                                           | The acceptance tests' clock: a request may name the time the API reads with `X-Test-Now`. Mounted only under `QANDO_TEST_CLOCK=on`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

**Depends on:** `packages/contracts`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`, `apps/api/src/email`, `apps/api/src/modules/auth`, `apps/api/src/modules/date-interpretations`, `apps/api/src/modules/management`, `apps/api/src/modules/participations`, `apps/api/src/modules/public-events`, `apps/api/src/modules/results`.

### `apps/api/src/modules`

**Summary:** One folder per API resource.

**Purpose:** Each module is a router built from a port (its view of the database) and the clock; the repository behind the port lives in the same folder. Routers never touch the database directly, so their unit tests replace the port.

**Contents:** `auth/`, `date-interpretations/`, `docs/`, `health/`, `management/`, `participations/`, `public-events/`, `results/`.

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/email`, `apps/api/src/phrase-editor`, `packages/contracts`, `packages/date-reader`, `packages/domain`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/auth`

**Summary:** Sign-in by email code, sessions, the session request and sign-out, through Better Auth.

**Purpose:** The only place that knows Better Auth: its Drizzle adapter on the account tables of `apps/api/src/db` and its email-code plugin. Codes have 6 digits, are stored hashed, last 10 minutes and 5 wrong tries, and a new one replaces the last; the first code an email signs in with creates its account, already verified. Only sign-in codes are sent (qando has no passwords to reset). A session is the cookie `qando.session_token` (`__Secure-qando.session_token` over HTTPS): HttpOnly, SameSite=Lax, Secure over HTTPS, valid 90 days from its last use and renewed at most once a day. Better Auth's Origin check stays on in every environment (it skips it under `NODE_ENV=test` otherwise). None of Better Auth's own routes is served: qando's routes call it behind their own Origin checks (`sessionOriginCheck`, `ownPagesOnly`). Sign-out is ours because Better Auth's logs a failed deletion and still answers success; sending the code is ours so that a failed delivery reaches the page (`502`) and the email is qando's own, and signing in is ours so that a wrong code answers the tries left, which Better Auth keeps but does not answer.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                   | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `createAuth(dependencies)`, `Auth` (`auth.ts`)                           | Better Auth over qando's database, under `/api/auth`. Its own code sending is never reached: its callback rejects.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `AuthDependencies` (`auth.ts`)                                           | The database, the secret that signs session cookies, the public address of the pages (Better Auth trusts only pages of its origin), and `log(entry)`, where Better Auth's warnings and failures go.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `AuthLogEntry` (`auth.ts`)                                               | What is logged of a Better Auth warning or failure, or of a sign-in code not delivered: its level, its fixed message (`Authentication problem`, `Sign-in code not delivered`) and `describeError` of its error, never Better Auth's message or arguments (a failed query quotes the session token or the email).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `signInCodesOf(auth, delivery)`, `SignInCodeDelivery` (`auth.ts`)        | The `SignInCodes` port over Better Auth: it creates, stores (hashed) and checks the codes; the code is delivered with `delivery.sendEmail` (the `SendEmail` port of `apps/api/src/email`) in `signInCodeEmail`, greeting the account by its stored name, and a failed delivery goes to `delivery.log` and changes nothing: an email keeps a single stored code, and the new one (created by Better Auth, then set aside) takes the place of the last delivered one only once its email was delivered. Each swap is one transaction, and an email's swaps and tries run in turns (in memory), so none meets a code half swapped. After a wrong code, the tries left are read from the code Better Auth stores (`<hash>:<wrong tries>`): a change of that format in an upgrade fails loudly, never with a wrong count. |
| `signInRouter(codes, sessions, now)` (`sign-in.ts`)                      | `POST /api/auth/sign-in-code` and `POST /api/auth/sign-in`, only to qando's pages (`ownPagesOnly`), never cached. Sending is limited per email by `sendQuota` on the clock given, the API's (a new code after 30 seconds, at most 5 in any hour; a code the email server refused gives its place back, so it can be retried at once) and to 20 an hour per address; tries to 30 per 15 minutes per address, besides the 5 wrong tries each code allows.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `SignInCodes`, `CodeDelivery`, `SignInOutcome` (`sign-in.ts`)            | The port the router signs in through: `send(email)` creates a code and delivers it (only a delivered code replaces the email's last one), answering `sent` or `not-delivered` (never throwing for a failed delivery); `signIn(email, code, name?)` answers `signed-in` with the session cookies (the account created if new, with `name` if given; an existing account keeps its own), `wrong-code` with the tries left, `expired` (expired, used or never sent) or `too-many-attempts`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `signInCodeEmail(data)`, `SignInCodeEmailData` (`sign-in-code-email.ts`) | The email that carries a code, in Spanish, in the emails' shared frame: the code in the subject (`<code> es tu código de qando`, readable in a phone notification) and in the body with its 10-minute expiry, which is also its preview text; "Si no lo has pedido tú, ignora este mensaje." as its footer; the account's stored name in the greeting when it has one (never a name sent by a browser). |
| `sessionsOf(auth)` (`auth.ts`)                                           | The `Sessions` port of `apps/api/src/http` over Better Auth: the signed cookie, its account (ID, name and email), the cookie renewed while reading it (which Better Auth only returns to our routes), and ending a session: deleted first, so a failure rejects, then its cookies cleared.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `signOutRouter(sessions)` (`sign-out.ts`)                                | `POST /api/auth/sign-out`: after `sessionOriginCheck`, ends the session and answers `200 { signedOut: true }` with the cookies cleared, never cached; a session that could not be deleted answers `500` and keeps its cookie, so the page can retry.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `sessionRouter(sessions)` (`session.ts`)                                 | `GET /api/auth/session`, which every page asks to know whether its browser is signed in: `200 { name, email }` of the session's account with the cookie renewed while reading it, or `200 null` without a session or with one that expired or was signed out (never `401`); never cached. A request without the cookie costs no database work; a session that could not be read answers `500`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/email`, `apps/api/src/http`, `packages/contracts/src/auth`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/date-interpretations`

**Summary:** Read the dates and times a phrase says.

**Purpose:** Serves `POST /api/date-interpretations`: the dates and times a phrase in plain language says, for the page that creates an event to mark. The two parts that interpret a date phrase meet in `phraseInterpreter`, which knows nothing of HTTP, so another way in (an agent's tool) can call it and leave the copy edit out: the phrase is copy-edited (`EditPhrase` of `apps/api/src/phrase-editor`, which answers it as written when the model is slow, fails or changes what it says) and then read by the rules reader (`readDates` of `packages/date-reader`), with today and the time now taken in the request's time zone. Every phrase gets an answer: what is not understood comes as notices, never as an error. Only qando's own pages are answered (`403 FORBIDDEN` otherwise), 20 times in any hour per client address (`429 RATE_LIMITED`), counted before anything is read, so a refusal never reaches the model; a body above 2 kB is refused unread (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). The phrase is neither stored nor logged, and no answer is cached. The module has no port: it reads no database.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                               | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `dateInterpretationsRouter(interpret, sessions)` (`date-interpretations.ts`)                         | The router of `POST /api/date-interpretations`: `noStore`, `ownPagesOnly` (`403`), `rollingHourLimit` of 20 in any hour per address (`429`), the body read against the contract (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`), then `interpret` of the body's phrase, time zone and region, with the copy edit; answers `200 { options, questions, notices }`.                                                                                                  |
| `phraseInterpreter(editPhrase, now)`, `InterpretPhrase`, `PhraseToInterpret` (`interpret-phrase.ts`) | The interpreter of date phrases, apart from HTTP: `InterpretPhrase` takes `{ text, timeZone, region?, copyEdit }` and answers the reading of `readDates` as data. With `copyEdit` the phrase is first copy-edited by `editPhrase`; without it the phrase is read as given and the language model is not asked (a caller whose phrase is already well written). `today` (YYYY-MM-DD) and `now` (HH:MM) are those of the clock in `timeZone`. |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/phrase-editor` (the type `EditPhrase`), `packages/contracts/src/date-interpretations`, `packages/date-reader`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

**Summary:** The OpenAPI document and Swagger UI.

**Purpose:** Serves `/api/openapi.json`, generated from the contract, and Swagger UI at `/api/docs` to browse it.

**Exports:**

| Export                     | What it does                                       |
| -------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------- |
| `docsRouter()` (`docs.ts`) | The router of `/api/openapi.json` and `/api/docs`. |

**Depends on:** `packages/contracts`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/health`

**Summary:** `GET /api/health`.

**Purpose:** Says whether the API and its database answer, for the hosting's health check and the home page.

**Exports:**

| Export                                      | What it does                                                                                 |
| ------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `healthRouter(checkDatabase)` (`health.ts`) | Answers `200 { status: ok, database: up }` or `503 { status: unavailable, database: down }`. |
| `DatabaseCheck` (`health.ts`)               | The port: resolves to true when the database answers.                                        |

**Depends on:** `packages/contracts`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/events`

**Summary:** Create an event with the session.

**Purpose:** Serves `POST /api/events`. Only a session creates: none, or an ended one, answers `401 UNAUTHENTICATED` before the body is read, and the session cookie from a page of another site or without `Origin` `403 FORBIDDEN`. The body is checked by `validateNewEvent` (`packages/domain/src`) with today in the event's time zone; every problem is answered at once (`400 VALIDATION_FAILED`). The event is owned by the session's account, which keeps its email; the management token comes from the page in `X-Management-Token` and only its hash is stored; a send that repeats the token of an event of the same account stores nothing and answers that event again, and another account's token is refused (`MANAGEMENT_TOKEN_TAKEN`). Body limit 16 kB, never cached. The first event of a browser signs in through `apps/api/src/modules/auth` first.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                            | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `createEventRouter(newEvents, sessions, now)` (`create-event.ts`) | The HTTP side: the session, the token header and the body, the domain's check, `201 { participationId, resultsId }` or the refusal.                                                                                               |
| `NewEvents` (`create-event.ts`)                                   | The port: `create(ownerId, managementToken, event)` stores a valid new event for its owner and answers its IDs; the IDs of the event the token already holds when the owner's (nothing stored), `taken` when another account's.                                                                                                           |
| `createNewEvents(db, now)` (`new-events-repository.ts`)           | The PostgreSQL implementation, one transaction: the owner's account locked and named after the organizer if it has no name yet, the event with two new links and the token's hash (an insert that meets the hash stores nothing and answers the event that holds it), its summary email queued to the account's email, due at the clock's time, and its options with their position in the order the domain gave. |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/email`, `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/capability-token.ts`, `apps/api/src/modules/auth`, `packages/contracts/src/management`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`, `apps/api/src/test`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/management`

**Summary:** Manage an event with its management token or its owner's session; list the owner's events.

**Purpose:** Serves the management endpoints under `/api/events/:id`, `:id` being the participation ID of the link; `GET`, the event as its organizer manages it, `POST …/confirmation`, confirming its final option, and `POST …/emails/:type/retry`, trying a failed email once more. Also "Mis eventos", `GET /api/me/events`: the events of the session's account, newest first, each in its current state (`401` without a session; a cancelled event is no longer listed, its remains have no owner). Who is acting is resolved in one place, `actingOrganizer`: every management use case calls it first and only gets on with an `ActingOrganizer`, which only that function makes, so the owner's session is one more way to resolve it (later an agent's grant too) without changing any use case; either credential that manages the event grants it. No credential answers `401 UNAUTHENTICATED` (a session that ended counts as none), credentials that do not manage the event (another event's token, any other link, the session of another account, an unknown event) `403 FORBIDDEN`, a cancelled event `410 EVENT_CANCELLED` with its title. The token travels in `X-Management-Token`, is matched by its hash and never logged; the session's account comes from `signedInAccount` (`apps/api/src/http`).

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                                                | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `actingOrganizer(managedEvents, participationId, credentials)` (`managed-events.ts`)                                  | Resolves the request's credentials into the organizer of the link's event, or the refusal; no credential costs no database work.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `ActingOrganizer`, `ManagementCredentials`, `ManagementAccess`, `ManagementRefusal` (`managed-events.ts`)             | The organizer allowed to manage one event (its stored ID), what a request carries (the token, the signed-in account), and the access granted or refused (unauthenticated, forbidden, cancelled).                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `ManagementMatch` (`managed-events.ts`)                                                                               | What the stored event is matched by: the token's hash, the owner's account, or both; its type never holds neither.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ManagedEvents` (`managed-events.ts`)                                                                                 | The port: `access(participationId, match)`, `ownedBy(accountId)`, the account's events newest first, `event(organizer)`, the event an organizer manages, `retryFailedEmail(organizer, type, at)`, which makes its failed email due again and answers whether it had failed, and `change(work)`, which runs a change to it in one transaction.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `EventChange`, `LockedEvent` (`managed-events.ts`)                                                                    | The reads and writes of one change: `lock(organizer)` locks the event and answers its option IDs and confirmed option (undefined if deleted since the check), `confirm(organizer, optionId)` stores the confirmation and closes voting.                                                                                                                               |
| `StoredManagedEvent` (`managed-events.ts`)                                                                            | What the management page is computed from: the event, its options, confirmation, links, every vote's states and its queued emails (`emails`). |
| `StoredEmail` (`managed-events.ts`) | One email queued to the organizer: its type and its status (pending, sent with its instant, or failed).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `StoredOwnEvent` (`managed-events.ts`) | What the account's list is computed from: the participation ID, title, deadline, creation instant and stored state of one of its events. |
| `readManagedEvent(managedEvents, participationId, credentials, now)`, `ManagedEventReading` (`read-managed-event.ts`) | The use case, free of HTTP: the event with its current state, vote count, recommended options and emails, or the refusal; a pending "voting closed" is `scheduled` while voting is open (it waits for the closing), being sent once it has closed. An event deleted between the check and the read answers forbidden.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `managedEventFor(managedEvents, organizer, now)` (`read-managed-event.ts`)                                            | The same reading once access is granted, for a use case that answers the event after changing it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `confirmOption(managedEvents, participationId, credentials, optionId, now)`, `Confirmation` (`confirm-option.ts`)     | The use case behind confirming, free of HTTP: before or after the deadline, checked and stored with the event locked; the option ID is lower-cased before it is checked and stored; the event as it is now, or the refusal (those of `actingOrganizer`, `already-confirmed`, `not-an-option`). The confirmed option sent again answers the event and changes nothing. |
| `retryEmail(managedEvents, participationId, credentials, type, now)`, `EmailRetry` (`retry-email.ts`) | The use case behind "Reintentar", free of HTTP: the acting organizer first, then the failed email due again, answered as pending; `not-failed` when it was sent, is still being tried or was never queued, or the refusal of `actingOrganizer`. |
| `managementRouter(managedEvents, now)` (`management.ts`)                                                              | The HTTP side: reads the path, the token header, the signed-in account and the body (`400` names each broken part, never its value), answers each outcome with its status (`not-failed` as `409 EMAIL_NOT_FAILED`), never cached.                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `listMyEvents(managedEvents, accountId, now)` (`list-my-events.ts`) | The use case behind "Mis eventos", free of HTTP: the account's events, newest first, each with its current state (`currentState`). |
| `myEventsRouter(managedEvents, sessions, now)` (`my-events.ts`) | The HTTP side of `GET /api/me/events`: the session (`401` without one, before anything is read), the list as the contract shows it, never cached. |
| `createManagedEvents(db)` (`managed-events-repository.ts`)                                                            | The PostgreSQL implementation: the token matched by its hash and the session by the event's owner, in the query; the account's events by its owner index, newest first; only a refusal costs the read that tells a cancelled event apart. A change locks the event row `FOR UPDATE`, which waits for the votes holding it `FOR SHARE`. The event's emails are read the summary first; a confirmation drops the "voting closed" not sent (`dropUnsentVotingClosed`); a retry goes through `retryFailedEmail` (`apps/api/src/email`), the contract's `voting-closed` stored as `voting_closed`.                                                                                       |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/capability-token.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/management`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/participations`

**Summary:** Cast, read and edit a vote.

**Purpose:** Serves the voting page's sends and reads under `/api/public/events/:id/participations`: cast a vote (`POST`), read the vote of a token (`GET …/mine`) and replace it (`PUT …/mine`). Each send runs in one transaction that locks the event, so the organizer cannot close voting or change the options in between; a repeated token stores no second vote.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                | What it does                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `participationsRouter(participations, now)` (`participations.ts`)                     | The router of the three endpoints; checks the event is open (`currentState`) and the vote valid (`validateVote`) before storing.                                      |
| `Participations` (`participations.ts`)                                                | The port: `transaction(work)` runs a `Ballot` in one transaction.                                                                                                     |
| `Ballot` (`participations.ts`)                                                        | The reads and writes of one vote: `event(publicId)` (locked), `participation(tokenHash)`, `add(eventId, tokenHash, vote)`, `replace(eventId, participationId, vote)`. |
| `StoredVotingEvent`, `VotingEventLookup`, `StoredParticipation` (`participations.ts`) | What the port answers: the event found, cancelled or missing; a stored participation.                                                                                 |
| `createParticipations(db)` (`participations-repository.ts`)                           | The PostgreSQL implementation of the port.                                                                                                                            |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/capability-token.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/public-events`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/public-events`

**Summary:** Read an event by its participation link.

**Purpose:** Serves `GET /api/public/events/:id`: what a participant may see of an event (never the organizer's email, tokens, counts or votes), with its current state.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                        | What it does                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `publicEventsRouter(publicEvents, now)` (`public-events.ts`)  | Answers the event with `currentState`, `404 NOT_FOUND` or `410 EVENT_CANCELLED` with its title.       |
| `PublicEvents` (`public-events.ts`)                           | The port: `find(publicId)`.                                                                           |
| `StoredPublicEvent`, `PublicEventLookup` (`public-events.ts`) | The stored event in the shape participants see, and what a link leads to (found, cancelled, missing). |
| `createPublicEvents(db)` (`public-events-repository.ts`)      | The PostgreSQL implementation; reads only the columns participants may see.                           |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/http`, `packages/contracts/src/public-events`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/modules/results`

**Summary:** Read the results by the results link.

**Purpose:** Serves `GET /api/results/:resultsId`: the domain's results (`resultsOf`) with each shown option in full and, on named events, the names of who answered it each way in Spanish alphabetical order (a name repeated by a later vote reads "Ana (2)"); final once voting is no longer open (`currentState`). The organizer reaches it from the management page (which "Mis eventos" opens) through the same results link, so no other credential opens it. The participation link of an event answers `403 FORBIDDEN`, a cancelled event `410 EVENT_CANCELLED` with its title, an unknown link `404 NOT_FOUND`.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                            | What it does                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `readResults(eventResults, resultsId, now)`, `ResultsReading` (`read-results.ts`) | The use case, free of HTTP so every adapter reads the same results: the results as the page shows them, or why there are none (cancelled, a participation link, missing). |
| `EventResults` (`read-results.ts`)                                                | The port: `find(resultsId)`.                                                                                                                                              |
| `StoredResultsEvent`, `StoredVote`, `ResultsLookup` (`read-results.ts`)           | The event with its options and every vote; what a link leads to (found, cancelled, a participation link, missing).                                                        |
| `resultsRouter(eventResults, now)` (`results.ts`)                                 | The HTTP side of `readResults`: reads the path, answers each reading with its status, never cached.                                                                       |
| `createEventResults(db)` (`results-repository.ts`)                                | The PostgreSQL implementation: one read of the event with its options, confirmation and votes; only a link that opens no results costs the reads that tell why.           |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/http`, `packages/contracts/src/results`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`.

### `apps/api/src/phrase-editor`

**Summary:** The copy edit of a date phrase by a language model, and its limits.

**Purpose:** The first of the two parts that interpret a date phrase: before the rules reader reads it, a language model rewrites the phrase into a well-written one in the same language, so that "el vienres alas 9" is read as "el viernes a las 9". The model never reads dates and is never trusted. This folder is the edge to it and everything that keeps it harmless: the port the model sits behind, its one implementation with Google's Gemini API, the 3-second limit and the check of its answer in code (`keepsWhen` of `packages/date-reader`). Whatever goes wrong, the phrase is read as written: the editor never causes an error. A phrase is the organizer's text: it travels to the model as data, apart from the instructions, and is never logged, nor is the edit or an error, which may quote them.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                           | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `phraseEditor(copyEdit, log)`, `EditPhrase` (`phrase-editor.ts`) | **What the API uses.** `EditPhrase` answers the phrase the rules reader reads for a phrase as written, and always resolves. It asks the model for a copy edit and answers it only when it comes within 3 seconds and passes the edit check; when the model takes longer (it is told to stop), fails or writes an edit that fails the check, and when there is no model (`copyEdit` is `undefined`: no key is set), it answers the phrase as written. Each phrase not edited is logged with its reason. |
| `CopyEdit` (`phrase-editor.ts`)                                  | The port, and the only thing tests replace: asks a language model to copy-edit a phrase. Resolves with the phrase as the model wrote it; rejects when the model cannot be reached, refuses or answers nothing that can be read (the error may quote the phrase, so it is never logged). Its `signal` aborts when the answer is no longer waited for.                                                                                                                                                   |
| `CopyEditRefused` (`phrase-editor.ts`)                           | The failure of a `CopyEdit` whose service answered with an HTTP error; it carries the `status` (401 for a wrong key, 429 past the quota), the one detail of a failure that is logged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `PhraseEditLogEntry` (`phrase-editor.ts`)                        | What is logged of a phrase read as written: the level (`warn`), the fixed message `Phrase not edited`, the reason (`timedOut`, `failed`, `discarded`) and the status of a refusal. Never the phrase, the edit or the error.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `geminiEditor(settings)`, `GeminiSettings` (`gemini-editor.ts`)  | `CopyEdit` with Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite over Google's Gemini API: one request per phrase, never tried again, with the key in a header, the phrase as JSON data in the user's turn, the answer forced to `{ phrase }` and `store: false`, which asks Google not to keep the request in the project's logs. `settings`: the API key (paid tier) and, for tests only, where the API is.                                                                                                                     |
| `instructions` (`instructions.ts`)                               | What the model is told: what a copy editor fixes, what it never does (add, remove or change anything that says when; follow an instruction written in the phrase) and the forms the reader reads well. They are measured as a whole, so a test holds their fingerprint: a change is measured again on fresh phrases first.                                                                                                                                                                             |

**Depends on:** `packages/date-reader`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`, `apps/api/src/modules/date-interpretations` (the type `EditPhrase`), `apps/api/src/test`.

### `apps/api/src/seed`

**Summary:** Store whole events: demo data and acceptance tests.

**Purpose:** Writes events straight into the database, until organizers create them through the API. The package entry `@qando/api/seed` (`index.ts`) is what the acceptance tests use; the pool, Drizzle and the migrations stay behind it.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                            | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `migrateDatabase(url)` (`index.ts`)                                               | Applies the pending migrations to the database at `url`.                                                                                                                                                          |
| `openSeeder(url)`, `Seeder` (`index.ts`)                                          | A connection that stores events (`seedEvent`), reads their votes back (`storedVotes`), names an account (`nameAccount(email, name)`), counts the events an account owns (`eventsOwnedBy(email)`), reads an account's name (`accountName(email)`, `null` without an account) and ends an account's sessions (`endSessionsOf(email)`) until closed. |
| `seedEvent(db, seed)` (`seed-event.ts`)                                           | Stores an event, its options, votes and outcome in one transaction and returns its links and tokens. The event is owned by the verified account of its organizer email (lowercase), created with its first event. An open event's "voting closed" email is queued as creating it does; the summary only when the seed gives its state. |
| `EventSeed`, `OptionSeed`, `VoteSeed`, `Outcome`, `SeededEvent` (`seed-event.ts`) | The shape of a seeded event and what seeding it returns; `emails.summary` (`sent` or `failed`) stores the summary as the outbox would leave it (1 or 5 attempts).                                                                                                                                                          |
| `storedVotes(db, publicId)` (`stored-votes.ts`)                                   | The votes stored for an event, in the shape they are seeded, so a test can check what a vote stored.                                                                                                              |
| `demoEvents(today)`, `DemoEvent`, `linksOf(…)` (`demo-events.ts`)                 | The demo events of `npm run db:seed`, one per state the pages show, dated from `today`, and their labelled links.                                                                                                 |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/db`, `apps/api/src/capability-token.ts`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src/seed.ts`, `e2e/steps`.

### `apps/web/public/background`

**Summary:** The drawing behind every page, in four versions.

**Purpose:** The drawing (green waves on cream paper) is large, so each screen downloads only the version it shows, chosen by `apps/web/src/styles` (`_page-background.scss`) from the screen's shape and density.

**Contents:** `portrait-800.webp` (upright screens, the phones), `portrait-1200.webp` (upright screens from 600 px wide, the tablets), `landscape-1920.webp` (screens on their side), `landscape-2560.webp` (screens on their side from 1024 px wide and dense, or wider than 1920 px).

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/styles`.

### `apps/web/public/email`

**Summary:** Images the emails show.

**Purpose:** An email links its images by their full address, so the site serves them at `/email/…`, as PNG (many email clients do not show SVG). Mail clients keep a copy of each image by its address (Gmail's and Apple Mail's proxies), so each file carries in its name the first 8 hex characters of its content's SHA-256: a new image is a new file, and the email that links it changes its address. The API and the site deploy separately, so a new file reaches the site in a pull request of its own before an email links it; otherwise an email sent between the two deploys links a file the site does not have yet.

**Contents:** `logo-c75530c8.png` (qando's symbol, the two shapes without the word; twice the size the emails show it, for sharp screens).

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/api/src/email` (the emails' shared frame links it).

Each page loads on demand. |

### `apps/web/src/app/core`

**Summary:** App-wide services.

**Purpose:** What every page relies on and exists once in the app: access to the API, the session and what goes with it (the private pages' guard, the management tokens kept, where signing in comes back to), translations, the header.

### `apps/web/src/app/core/api`

**Summary:** Typed access to the API contract.

**Purpose:** Pages name an endpoint of the contract and get its typed, schema-checked answer; a contract change breaks the build of every page that uses it.

**Exports:**

| Export                                              | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `apiResource(endpoint, params)` (`api-resource.ts`) | Reads a GET endpoint as a resource that follows the page: read again when its parameters change; its value is the checked success body.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `apiError(endpoint, error)` (`api-resource.ts`)     | The documented error answer behind a failed request, typed by its status; undefined when the API was not reached or the answer is not in the contract.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ApiClient` (`api-client.ts`)                       | `send(endpoint, request)` for the actions a page takes (send a vote): resolves with the checked success body, rejects with the HTTP error, or with a timeout once the time the request was given (`within`) has passed, the request given up.                                                                                                                             |
| `newCapabilityToken()` (`capability-token.ts`) | A new capability token made by the page before its first send (a vote's, an event's): 128 random bits from the browser in base64url, so a repeated send carries the same token and stores nothing twice. |
| `ApiRequest` (`api-client.ts`)                      | What a request carries: path parameters, headers and body, typed by the endpoint; and, optionally, `within`, the milliseconds the answer is waited for before the request is given up (no limit when absent).                                                                                                                                                             |
| `requestFailure(error)` (`request-failure.ts`)      | Follows the error a request or a resource failed with as a `RequestFailure` (null without one): a `429 RATE_LIMITED` counts its `Retry-After` seconds down each second, from the clock, and stays at 0; any other error, or a `429` that breaks the contract, is a plain failure. Runs in an injection context.                                                           |
| `RequestFailure` (`request-failure.ts`)             | How a request failed, as a page tells it: `failed` (trying again may work now) or `limited` with the `seconds` still to wait.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `waiting(failure)` (`request-failure.ts`)           | A rate limit with seconds left: sending again now would be refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `secondsUntil(until)` (`wait.ts`)                   | The whole seconds left until a clock time in milliseconds (null: nothing to wait for), as a signal counted down each second from the clock, so a tab whose timers were held back still shows the time left; 0 once the time has come, and without one; a new time starts again. `requestFailure` counts with it. Runs in an injection context, whose end stops the count. |

**Depends on:** `packages/contracts`.

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app/core/auth`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, `apps/web/src/app/features/manage`, `apps/web/src/app/features/my-events`, `apps/web/src/app/features/results`, `apps/web/src/app/features/sign-in`, `apps/web/src/app/features/vote`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`.

### `apps/web/src/app/core/auth`

**Summary:** The session, the private pages' guard, the management tokens kept and where signing in comes back to.

**Purpose:** What the browser knows of its organizer: which account is signed in (asked to the API, as the page cannot read the session cookie), signing out, the guard that keeps the private pages for an account signed in, and the management tokens the browser keeps until it signs out. Also the one place that knows how a page asks the sign-in page to come back to it (the `returnTo` query of `/sign-in`) and which page signing in opens, so the link that asks and the page that answers cannot disagree. Only a path of qando is ever followed.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                     | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `signInFrom(url)` (`sign-in-return.ts`)                                    | The query of a link to the sign-in page from the page at `url` (path and query), so that signing in comes back to it: `{ returnTo }`, nothing from the home page, and from the sign-in page what it was already asked.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `returnTarget(returnTo)` (`sign-in-return.ts`)                             | The page to open after signing in, from the sign-in page's `returnTo`: that page when it is one of qando's other than the home and the sign-in pages, "Mis eventos" (`/my-events`) otherwise; an address of another site is never followed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `Session` (`session.ts`)                                                   | The organizer's session on this browser. `account`: a signal of the account signed in (`SignedInAccount`, `packages/contracts/src/auth`), `undefined` until the API first answers (`GET /api/auth/session`, asked when the app starts), `null` without a session. `signedIn()`: whether an account is signed in, once the API has answered; a question the API could not answer counts as no session and is asked again here. `refresh()`: asks the API again, as after signing in. `signOut()`: the server ends the session (`POST /api/auth/sign-out`), then the browser forgets the account and every management token it keeps (`ManagementTokens.forgetAll`; the voters' tokens stay); rejects, with nothing forgotten, when the server could not end it. Only the answer to the last question or sign-out is kept. The browser's tabs share the session: a tab that signs in (`refresh()`) or out tells the others (`BroadcastChannel`), which ask again or forget as if it had happened in them. |
| `signedInOnly` (`signed-in-only.ts`), guard of `/my-events` and `/profile` | Opens the private pages for an account signed in (`Session.signedIn`); sends anyone else to `/sign-in?returnTo=<the page asked for>` (`signInFrom`), so signing in comes back to it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `ManagementTokens` (`management-tokens.ts`)                                | The management tokens this browser keeps, one per event whose management link it opened: in its storage (shared by its tabs, kept after a reload), or for the page alone when the browser refuses its storage (private windows, blocked site data). `keep(eventId, token)` keeps the event's token in the place of the one kept before; `of(eventId)` answers it, `null` when none is kept; `forgetAll()` forgets every one, in every tab, as signing out does, so a shared computer keeps no way into anyone's events (the links stay in their emails).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Depends on:** `apps/web/src/app/core/api`, `packages/contracts`.

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app` (routes), `apps/web/src/app/core/layout`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, `apps/web/src/app/features/manage`, `apps/web/src/app/features/my-events`, `apps/web/src/app/features/sign-in`.

| Export                                         | What it does                                                                                      |

| `I18nTitleStrategy` (`i18n-title-strategy.ts`) | Turns a route's `title` key into the browser tab's "<page> \| qando", or "qando" without a title. |
