| `optionInFull(option, locale, translate)`, `Translate` (`display.ts`)                       | An option as a confirmed date is named: "viernes 10 de julio de 2026, 20:00 a 22:00", with weekday and year of each day and its times; `translate` reads the catalogue's shapes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `zoneCity(timeZone)` (`display.ts`)                                                         | `America/New_York` → "New York", or null when the browser is in that zone (no notice due).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `capitalised(name, locale)` (`display.ts`)                                                  | "ana" → "Ana".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

**Depends on:** `apps/web/src/app/core/api`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`, `apps/web/src/styles`, `packages/contracts/src/public-events`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, `apps/web/src/app/features/manage`, `apps/web/src/app/features/my-events`, `apps/web/src/app/features/results`.

### `apps/web/src/app/shared`

**Summary:** Pieces several pages use.

**Purpose:** Groups the shared UI; a piece moves here once a second page needs it.

**Contents:** `ui/`.

**Depends on:** `apps/web/src/styles`.

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app`, `apps/web/src/app/features`.

### `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`

**Summary:** Small UI components and directives.

**Purpose:** Generic pieces with no knowledge of events or votes: loading, error and not-found states, the step stack, the toast, the long press, the tooltip, the folded scrolling box, the header of a column of times, the arrows of a step whose content comes in pages, the room a step's boxes have on the screen, the modal dialog, the share buttons, the words of a month calendar and what is drawn over the whole page.

**Components:**

| Component                                                                                                                                 | Inputs → outputs                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **`LoadingState`** (`loading-state.*`)                                                                                                    | `message`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | A spinner and a message ("Cargando…" by default) in a card while a page waits for its data.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **`ErrorState`** (`error-state.*`)                                                                                                        | `message`, `retryable`, `error` → `retry`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | A message when a page could not get its data, with a retry button when trying again can help. Given the error, a rate limit says "Hay demasiadas visitas desde tu red en este momento." and its button counts the seconds down ("Reintentar en 42 s"), disabled until 0 (`requestFailure`, `apps/web/src/app/core/api`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **`FailureNotice`** (`failure-notice.*`)                                                                                                  | `failure`, `failedKey`, `waitKey`, `limitedKey`                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | What a page says under the button whose request failed: the plain failure (`failedKey`), or what the limit is (`limitedKey`; by default, too many visits from the network) and the time left (`waitKey`, which receives `{seconds}` and, for a text that tells long waits in minutes, `{minutes}` rounded up with `{unit}`: `minutes` from one minute on, `seconds` below), gone at 0. Screen readers hear it once, with the seconds it starts with (a change inside an alert or an alertdialog is read again), the count shown going down hidden from them; the page styles it through its class on the element.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **`NotFoundCard`** (`not-found-card.*`)                                                                                                   | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | The card for an address that leads nowhere (no page of the app, or a link of no event): says so, why that may be, and links home; takes the focus to its heading.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **`StepStack`** (`step-stack.*`)                                                                                                          | `current` (two-way), `locked`; `focusCard()`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Steps as a stack of cards: the current card with its label, earlier steps folded into tabs that go back to them, and the motion between them (the card folds into its tab going forward, a tab grows back into the card going back while the later steps fade out; a change during a motion turns it from where it is); what a step holds is laid out in its card's width, never wider; moves the focus to the new card, and to the current card when a page asks (`focusCard`) after a change inside it that takes away the control pressed. A page sets `current`, and calls `focusCard` for such a change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **`Step`** directive (`step-stack.*`), `ng-template[appStep]`                                                                             | `appStep` (label), `accent`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | One step of a `StepStack`, with the colour of its dot (`StepAccent`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **`Toast`** (`toast.*`)                                                                                                                   | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Where the `Toaster`'s messages appear, once in the app shell, in a `status` region: dark, at the bottom of the screen, the message's button after its words. It tells the `Toaster` when the mouse or the focus is on the message.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| **`CharacterLimit`** directive (`character-limit.ts`), `input[formField][appCharacterLimit]` and `textarea[formField][appCharacterLimit]` | `appCharacterLimit` (a number)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Stops a text field or a text area of a Signal Form at that many characters as people see them (`clipBefore` of `@qando/domain`), once the spaces around are gone: what was just typed or pasted past it is dropped, the rest and the cursor kept, and the form's value follows; waits for a composed character (Chinese, Japanese) to be committed. No `maxlength`, which would cut an emoji in two.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **`SideSlide`** directive (`side-slide.ts`), `[appSideSlide]`; type `SlideEnd`                                                            | → `sliding` (pixels), `slid` (`SlideEnd`: `by`, pixels, and `flung`, `-1`, `0` or `1`)                                                                                                                                                                                      | A finger's slide sideways over the element. It begins once the finger has moved more than 10 px from where it was put down, more sideways than up or down: `sliding` says then and on each move how far sideways the finger is from where it was put down (less than nothing to the left), and `slid` where it lifted and whether it was flung: moving faster than 0.4 px each millisecond sideways over its last 100 ms, from its first move of that time to the lift, `-1` to the left and `1` to the right (the calendar of `WizardDates` turns its month with it; the columns of `ColumnStrip`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, do not read it). A finger that goes up or down first is left to the browser, to scroll with (`touch-action: pan-y pinch-zoom` on the element), and a touch whose move something inside has kept from the browser (a drag of its own, as `RowDrag`'s) is left alone until it ends. While it slides the page does not move with it. A slide the browser takes away, or that a second finger joins, ends with `slid` at 0, not flung.                                                   |
| **`LongPress`** directive (`long-press.ts`), `[appLongPress]`, exported as `appLongPress`                                                 | → `longPress`; signal `holding()`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Fires after a finger rests `HOLD_MS` (500 ms) on the element, with a short vibration, and swallows the click that follows (never the click of a key pressed on the element); exposes the `holding` class for a progress style (the `hold-ring` mixin, `apps/web/src/styles`), and `holding()` says a finger is resting to who draws the progress elsewhere (`HoldRing`, with the directive taken from the template as `#rest="appLongPress"`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **`HoldRepeat`** directive (`hold-repeat.ts`), `[appHoldRepeat]`                                                                          | `repeatWhile` (a function that answers whether to; always, by default) → `appHoldRepeat`                                                                                                                                                                                    | A button whose action repeats while it is held: fires as soon as a pointer presses the element, again after `REPEAT_DELAY_MS` and then every `REPEAT_EVERY_MS` until the pointer lifts, leaves the element or the window loses the focus; pressed with the keyboard or by assistive technology, once per press. The element needs no click handler of its own. While `repeatWhile` answers false it is a plain button, which fires once when a press ends on it (for an action that must not run before the pointer lifts, such as leaving a step); a hold under way ends there, and its press does not fire again when it lifts. It is asked at each press and before each repeat, not read when the page is drawn: the action itself may change the answer, and a busy page is not drawn again between two repeats.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **`DateChip`** (`date-chip.*`)                                                                                                            | `start`, `end` (`YYYY-MM-DD`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | A date or range as a pill named the short way ("Mié 8", "Mar 7➔Vie 10"), coloured by an `available`, `preferred` or `unavailable` class; only shows, the page puts it in a button to press it. `chipLabel(start, end, locale)` says what it shows in words ("Mar 7 – Vie 10"), for the name of that button to start with (WCAG 2.5.3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **`ColumnHeader`** (`column-header.*`)                                                                                                    | `start` (`YYYY-MM-DD`), `end` (the last day of a range of days, or `null`)                                                                                                                                                                                                  | The header of a column of times, a box as wide as its column: a single day as its month, its day and its weekday one over the other ("Jul", "6", "Lun", three letters each), a range of days as both ends, a little smaller and joined by "➔". It only shows: the page names the day for screen readers where it is answered, and puts the header in a button to press it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **`StepPager`** (`step-pager.*`)                                                                                                          | `page` (model, from 0), `pages` (how many), `backLabel`, `previousLabel`, `nextLabel`, `forwardLabel`, `forwardIcon` (`arrow` by default, or `check`), `held` (false by default) → `back`, `forward`                                                                        | The two round arrows that end a step whose content comes in pages, at the two sides of a row: they turn the pages, and only from the first and the last page do they leave the step (`back`, `forward`). Each is named and drawn after what it does next: the first, `backLabel` with an arrow on the first page and `previousLabel` with a chevron after it; the second, `nextLabel` with a chevron before the last page and `forwardLabel` on it, with an arrow or, for a step that ends the steps (`check`), a tick. Between them, only with several pages, the place among them ("2 / 3"), a small white pill (`count-pill`, `apps/web/src/styles`) red and bold on the last page. The step holds the page, to show it, and sets the room over the row. A step whose pages are short to turn (a column at a time) asks for arrows that are `held`: a page turns as soon as an arrow is pressed and another at each repeat while it stays down (`HoldRepeat`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`); the hold stops at the first or the last page, and leaving the step always takes a press of its own, which acts when it ends. |
| **`HoldRing`** (`hold-ring.*`)                                                                                                            | `anchor` (the element a finger rests on)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | The ring of a finger resting on its anchor for a long-press (`LongPress`), there while the finger rests: nothing for the first half of the rest, then a ring that closes on the anchor from outside the finger, closed when the long-press fires (only shown, without the motion, with reduced motion). Drawn over the whole page (`TopLayer`), as large as the anchor and where it is on screen when it comes; its corners as round as `--hold-ring-radius` (a little by default; round on the days of `WizardDates`). Used by `HourColumn` and `WizardDates` (`apps/web/src/app/features/create`).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **`TopLayer`** directive (`top-layer.ts`)                                                                                                 | —                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Draws its element over the whole page, free of everything around it, for as long as what wrote it shows it: in the browser's top layer (a manual popover), so that nothing cuts it (a box that hides what leaves it, a row drawn apart), and moved to the page's body, so that nothing holds it (the top layer alone is not enough on an iPhone, where a ring written inside the columns of the times step was drawn away from its hour). The element is placed in the viewport's coordinates (`position: fixed`), with what the browser gives a popover reset; what it inherits is the body's (every browser the app supports has a top layer).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **`Tooltip`** (`tooltip.*`)                                                                                                               | `anchor` (an element)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | A small white box above the anchor with a stem at its centre, showing what it is given; stays inside its container (the nearest positioned ancestor). Shown while it is in the page.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **`Switch`** (`switch.*`)                                                                                                                 | `on` (model), `label` (the name screen readers hear, for a switch whose name on screen is a drawing)                                                                                                                                                                        | A switch named by what it is given, a text or a drawing, shown before it: a white track with a round thumb, green with the thumb at its end once on. A press on the name or the track turns it, as the space bar does with the focus on it; screen readers hear a switch, its name and whether it is on (a checkbox with the `switch` role, which covers the track).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **`ScrollFold`** directive (`scroll-fold.ts`), `[appScrollFold]`, `exportAs: 'appScrollFold'`                                             | `appScrollFold` (how many children show, or `null`), `foldHeight` (the most it may measure, or `null`), `foldLeast` (the fewest a short `foldHeight` leaves in sight, one unless given), `foldStart` (the child at its top the first time it folds, the first unless given) | A box as tall as the children it shows (as many as fit in `foldHeight`, `foldLeast` at least) that scrolls the rest, measured again when a child joins or leaves: `back()` / `forward()` move one child (one further at each call, also while the box still slides from the call before), `nextHidden('back'                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | 'forward')`says which child they would bring into sight,`atStart`/`atEnd`say where it stands,`reveal(first, last)`brings those children into sight with the least scroll (nothing when they are whole in sight,`first`at the top when they start above it or do not all fit, else`last`at the bottom),`spare()`says how many pixels`foldHeight`leaves under the box as it is drawn (negative when even its fewest children pass it,`null`without a limit), for the page to put something there without taking a child from it, and`folded()`and the class`folded` say it scrolls, for the page's arrows and for its stylesheet to hide the scrollbar; the box has the class from the render in which it folds, so a stylesheet that lets it scroll only while folded loses nothing of what is asked of it in that render (its first place, a child to bring into sight). |
| **`Dialog`** (`dialog.*`)                                                                                                                 | `label`, `confirmLabel` → `closed`, `confirmed`                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | A card over the whole screen on a dark scrim (the browser's modal `<dialog>`), no wider than the page's column, with what it is given and a round ✓ at the bottom; open while it is in the page. The ✓, a tap on the scrim, Escape or back on a phone close it and emit `closed` for the page to remove it. Given a `confirmLabel`, it asks a question: "Volver" and that action (green) replace the ✓, and the action emits `confirmed` before closing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **`ShareLinks`** (`share-links.*`)                                                                                                        | `title`, `text`, `url`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Shares a link: "Compartir" opens the device's share sheet (without one, it points to the row under it), and the row opens WhatsApp or Telegram in a new tab, writes an email or copies the link (the `Toaster` confirms). Every channel carries the same title, text and link.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| **`PlaceholderPage`** (`placeholder-page.*`)                                                                                              | — (reads the route title)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Stands in for a view of the sitemap until its story is built: its title and "Esta página está en construcción." in a card.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                  | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `Toaster`, `ToastAction` (`toast.ts`)                                   | Short messages at the bottom of the screen, from any page, one at a time. `show(message)`: a message for two seconds ("Enlace copiado"). `offer(message, action)`: a message with a button for what can be done about it (`ToastAction`: the button's `label` and what a press does, `run`), for eight seconds; a press takes the message away and does it; answers a function that takes the message away early, which does nothing once the message is gone or another is in its place. `rest(what, resting)`, for `Toast`: while the mouse (`pointer`) or the focus (`focus`) is on a message it stays, and its whole time again after; a message that takes the place of another keeps what rested on that one, the mouse and, if both have a button, the focus. Signals `message()` and `action()`: what is on screen.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `StepAccent` (`step-stack.ts`)                                          | The identity colours of a step: `slate`, `blue`, `teal`, `amber`, `salmon`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `STEP_CARD` (`step-stack.ts`)                                           | The card of the current step of a `StepStack`, as a CSS selector: what a step holds finds the card it is in with `closest`, as `screenRoom` does to keep a step's card on screen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `HOLD_MS` (`long-press.ts`)                                             | How long the finger rests before a long press fires.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `REPEAT_DELAY_MS`, `REPEAT_EVERY_MS` (`hold-repeat.ts`)                 | How long a pointer is held before a `HoldRepeat` action begins to repeat (400 ms), and how often it repeats from then on (200 ms).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `screenMatches(query)` (`screen-matches.ts`)                            | Whether the screen matches a media query, now and as the screen turns or the window is resized, until the component it was called for is gone. Called in an injection context.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `TOUCH_QUERY` (`screen-matches.ts`)                                     | The media query of a device with no mouse, `(hover: none), (pointer: coarse)`: nothing hovers, or the pointer is a finger. Read once by the wizard's steps.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `screenRoom(host, parts, active)` (`screen-room.ts`)                    | The height, in pixels, that the boxes of a step may take for what ends the step (its buttons) to end above the bottom of the screen, 10 px free under it, with the page scrolled to its top, as a signal; `null` while `active()` is false or the parts are not on the page. `parts` are CSS selectors: `boxes`, inside `host`, which start at the same height, and `end`, what ends the step below them, inside `host` (its buttons) or around it (the step's card, `STEP_CARD`), and, if given, `aside`, boxes under the boxes whose height is left out of what goes below them (the tallest one's), for the page to show them only where the room spares them. Measured again after a render in which a signal read by `active` changed, when the window changes height and when the host, anything it is in or the page changes size, so also frame by frame while a step folds or grows above this one (where the browser has no `ResizeObserver`, not then). Called in an injection context; it stops listening with it. |
| `shapeOf(index, date, band, rows)`, `Shape`, `DayRows` (`day-shape.ts`) | How the day at `index` of a grid joins its neighbours in a band of days in a row from `band.start` to `band.end` (a range, an overlap, a painted choice): `joinsPrevious` / `joinsNext` when the neighbour is in the band and on the same row, `cutBefore` / `cutAfter` when the band goes on in another row or beyond the grid, `middle` when the day is neither its first nor its last. The grid's rows of seven start after its `leadingBlanks`; a band of one day joins nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `calendarWords(locale)`, `CalendarWords` (`calendar-words.ts`)          | The words of a month calendar in one language: `weekdays`, their initials from Monday on ("L", "M", "X"…); `monthTitle(month)`, a month (`YYYY-MM`) as a title with its year ("Julio 2026"); `dayName(date, today?)`, a date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) named in full ("lunes, 6 de julio"); `dayOfMonth(date, today?)`, a date without its weekday ("6 de julio"), both with its year when given a `today` of another year ("viernes, 8 de enero de 2027"); `shortDay(date)`, a date as its short weekday, capitalised, and its day ("Lun 6"); `monthName(month)`, a month (`YYYY-MM`) in full, as a list shows it ("noviembre"); `weekday(date)`, its weekday in full ("sábado"). Dates are calendar values, read as UTC.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |

**Depends on:** `apps/web/src/styles`, `apps/web/src/app/core/api`, `apps/web/src/app/core/i18n`, `packages/domain/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app`, `apps/web/src/app/core/layout`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, `apps/web/src/app/features/manage`, `apps/web/src/app/features/my-events`, `apps/web/src/app/features/not-found`, `apps/web/src/app/features/results`, `apps/web/src/app/features/sign-in`, `apps/web/src/app/features/vote`.

### `apps/web/src/i18n`

**Summary:** Translation catalogues.

**Purpose:** The UI copy as keyed ICU MessageFormat messages; no text is written in a template.

**Contents:** `es.json` (Spanish, shipped with the app).

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/web/src/app/core/i18n`.

### `apps/web/src/styles`

**Summary:** Design tokens, base styles and their checks.

**Purpose:** The colours, type, spacing, motion and breakpoints of the app (`docs/design-tokens.md`), and the tests that keep stylesheets on them: text contrast meets WCAG, and component stylesheets use tokens instead of literal values.

**Contents:** `_tokens.scss` (the design tokens), `_page-background.scss` (the drawing behind every page, `apps/web/public/background`: a fixed layer behind the content, so it stays still while the page scrolls, as tall as the largest viewport, so it does not jump when a phone's toolbar shows or hides; its version chosen by the screen's shape and density, the only one downloaded), `_base.scss` (element defaults, among them `data-strong-text`, which shows the bold parts of the catalogue's markup set with `[innerHTML]` in the text colour, since no component's styles reach that markup; the typed text of every text field, in the interface typeface, semibold, `--q-text-typed`, and its focus ring only under `data-keyboard-focus`, set by `provideKeyboardFocusRing` in `apps/web/src/app/core/layout`), `_breakpoints.scss` (media query mixins), `_controls.scss` (the look of controls the pages' steps share: `action-button`, a page's main action written out, a raised pill with bold text that sinks a little on hover and greys out disabled ("Enviar código", "Crear un evento"); `round-button`, the raised round arrow, flat and grey while `aria-disabled`; `corner-control`, a small white circle with a sign in it on a corner of the positioned element it acts on, sized and placed with `--round`, `--from-bottom` and `--from-end`, never pressed on less than `$least-target` (24 px) and hidden until `corner-control-shown`; `count-pill`, a small white pill, sunk in, that says how far along something is, as the place among a step's pages "2 / 5" (`StepPager`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`) and the wizard's options counter, red and bold at its end with `count-pill-at-end`; `tool-button`, a button of a step's row of tools, an icon with 40 px to press and no shape of its own, red while pressed or under the mouse; `fold-arrow`, ︿ or ﹀ at an end of a list that scrolls a row at a time, as wide as its list and as tall as its `--fold-size`, grey with the class `spent`), `_calendar.scss` (the look of the month calendar the pages' steps share: `card`, the white card around a month, which below 650 px reaches into the step card's padding; `month-title`, the month's name and year; `grid`, seven columns from Monday; `weekday`, a weekday's initial; `day-height`, for the component's `:host`, a day as tall as it is wide below 650 px and of a fixed height from there; `day-cell`, a day's number centred at that height; for the calendar where days are chosen, the wizard's dates, `header`, the row of the month's arrows and title, `month-arrow`, an arrow that changes the month, `picker-grid`, the grid with its rows further apart from 650 px, `weekdays-gap`, the weekdays as far from the days as a row from the next, `day-margin`, for the component's `:host`, a day's side margins, `day-place`, a day's place in the grid, and `day-button`, a day that can be chosen, a pill tinted under the mouse, pale and pressed by nothing when it cannot be chosen; `hold-ring`, for the `::after` of a positioned element a finger rests on (`LongPress`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`), a ring that fills around it until the long-press fires, with `hold-ring-motion` once in the same stylesheet, outside any rule); the test helpers below.

**Exports:**

| Export                                            | What it does                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `contrastRatio(text, background)` (`contrast.ts`) | The WCAG contrast ratio (1–21) of a text colour, translucent or not, over an opaque background. |
| `parseColor(css)`, `Rgba` (`contrast.ts`)         | Reads a hex or `rgb()`/`rgba()` colour.                                                         |
| `readCustomProperties(css)` (`contrast.ts`)       | The custom properties of a stylesheet with their `var()` references resolved.                   |
| `literalValues(css)` (`token-usage.ts`)           | The colours, lengths and durations written by hand in a stylesheet, which should be tokens.     |

These helpers are used only by the tests; they never reach the app bundle.

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/web/src`, `apps/web/src/app` (every component stylesheet).

### `apps/web/src/test`

**Summary:** Helpers for the web's own tests.

**Purpose:** What the browser has and jsdom does not, played by real objects the test controls. Left out of the app build and of mutation testing.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                                         | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `installTestScreen(matching)`, `TestScreen` (`media-queries.ts`)                                               | Replaces `window.matchMedia` with one that answers the given media queries (any other does not match); `set(query, matches)` changes an answer later and sends `change` to that query's lists, as when the screen turns wide.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `installTestRecognizer(name)`, `removeTestRecognizer()`, `TestRecognizer`, `TestRecognizers` (`recognizer.ts`) | The browser's speech recognizer, which jsdom has not: `installTestRecognizer` gives `window` one under its standard name (`SpeechRecognition`) or the prefixed one, and returns the list of the recognizers the page makes from then on; through each (`TestRecognizer`) the test sees how the page set it and left it (`lang`, `interimResults`, `continuous`, `state`) and has it say what the browser's would: `listens()`, `hears(...pieces)`, `fails(error)`, `ends()`. `removeTestRecognizer` leaves the browser without one. |

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** the web's `*.spec.ts` files.

### `packages`

**Summary:** Code shared by the API and the web.

**Purpose:** What both applications must agree on, written once: the contract of the API and the business rules; and the reader of dates written in plain language, which no application uses yet. No package depends on a framework, the database or an application (`eslint.config.js`).

**Contents:** `contracts/`, `date-reader/`, `domain/`.

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/api`, `apps/web`.

### `packages/contracts`

**Summary:** The API contract (`@qando/contracts`).

**Purpose:** Every endpoint defined once with its Zod schemas: the API reads requests and answers with them, the web uses their types and checks answers with them, and the OpenAPI document is generated from them. A test fails if the API does not serve an endpoint of the contract or answers outside its schemas.

**Contents:** `src/`; package, TypeScript, Vitest and Stryker settings.

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/api`, `apps/web`.

### `packages/contracts/src`

**Summary:** Endpoints, Zod schemas and OpenAPI generation.

**Purpose:** The machinery every endpoint uses, the shared error bodies, the calendar values endpoints share, and the list of endpoints. `index.ts` re-exports everything the applications import.

**Contents:** `auth/`, `date-interpretations/`, `health/`, `management/`, `public-events/`, `results/`; the files below.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `defineEndpoint(endpoint)`, `Endpoint`, `EndpointResponse`, `HttpMethod` (`endpoint.ts`)                                                                                                                               | Describes an endpoint (method, path, parameters, headers, body, responses) keeping its literal types.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `urlOf(endpoint, params)` (`endpoint.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                              | The endpoint's path with each `{placeholder}` replaced, URL-encoded.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `successBodyOf(endpoint, body)`, `errorResponseOf(endpoint, status, body)`, `isSuccessStatus(status)` (`endpoint.ts`)                                                                                                  | Checks a 2xx body against the success schemas (throws if it breaks them); finds the documented error answer of a status.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `SuccessBody`, `ErrorResponse`, `ParamsOf`, `HeadersOf`, `RequestBodyOf` (`endpoint.ts`)                                                                                                                               | The types of an endpoint's answers and request parts.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `errorBodySchema(code, shape)` (`error.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                            | The body every error shares: `{ error: { code, message, details } }`; `code` is what clients branch on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `internalErrorSchema`, `validationFailedSchema`, `fieldProblemSchema`, `unauthenticatedSchema`, `rateLimitedSchema`, `rateLimitedResponse`, `foreignPageSchema`, `ForeignPageBody`, `foreignPageResponse` (`error.ts`) | The shared error answers: `500`, `400` naming each broken part (never its value), `401` without any credential, `429`, and `403 FORBIDDEN` to a request only qando's pages may send (signing in, reading a date phrase) from a page of another site, or without `Origin`, session or not.                                                                                                                      |
| `InternalErrorBody`, `ValidationFailedBody`, `FieldProblem`, `RateLimitedBody` (`error.ts`)                                                                                                                            | The types of those bodies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `calendarDate`, `clockTime`, `timeZoneName` (`calendar-values.ts`)                                                                                                                                                     | The schemas of a calendar date (`YYYY-MM-DD`) and of a wall-clock time (`HH:MM`, 00:00 to 23:59), both with no zone: the schema that carries one says where it is read; and of an IANA time zone the runtime knows. Used inside the contract (`packages/contracts/src/public-events`, `packages/contracts/src/date-interpretations`, `packages/contracts/src/management`); `index.ts` does not re-export them. |
| `endpoints` (`endpoints.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                           | Every endpoint the API serves; the OpenAPI document lists exactly these.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `createOpenApiDocument()` (`openapi.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                               | Builds the OpenAPI document from the endpoints' schemas.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/api/src`, `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/modules`, `apps/web/src/app/core/api`.

### `packages/contracts/src/auth`

**Summary:** The sign-in, sign-out and session endpoints.

**Purpose:** `POST /api/auth/sign-in-code` sends a 6-digit code to an email; `POST /api/auth/sign-in` signs the browser in with it, creating the account if the email has none; `POST /api/auth/sign-out` deletes the browser's session on the server, then clears its cookies; `GET /api/auth/session` tells a page which account its browser is signed in as. Also the refusal of a session sent from a page of another site, shared by every change a session can make. Emails are trimmed and compared in lower case.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                         | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `sendSignInCodeEndpoint` (`sign-in.ts`)                                        | The endpoint that sends a code: `200` delivered, `400` a broken body, `403` from another site or without `Origin`, `429` too many codes (per email: a new one after 30 seconds, at most 5 an hour; per address), `502` not delivered. |
| `signInCodeBodySchema`, `SignInCodeBody` (`sign-in.ts`)                        | Its body `{ email }` (at most 254 characters).                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `signInCodeSentSchema`, `SignInCodeSent` (`sign-in.ts`)                        | Its answer `{ sent: true, resendAfterSeconds }`: the new code replaces the last one, valid for 10 minutes and 5 wrong tries.                                                                                                          |
| `codeNotDeliveredSchema`, `CodeNotDeliveredBody` (`sign-in.ts`)                | `502 CODE_NOT_DELIVERED`: the email server did not take the email; asking again sends a new code.                                                                                                                                     |
| `signInEndpoint` (`sign-in.ts`)                                                | The endpoint that signs in: `200` with the session cookie, `400` refused, `403` from another site, `429` too many tries from the address, `500`.                                                                                      |
| `signInBodySchema`, `SignInBody` (`sign-in.ts`)                                | Its body `{ email, code, name? }`, the code being 6 digits; `name`, trimmed, is the one an account created by this sign-in takes.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `signedInSchema`, `SignedIn` (`sign-in.ts`)                                    | Its answer `{ signedIn: true }`.                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `wrongCodeSchema`, `codeExpiredSchema`, `tooManyAttemptsSchema` (`sign-in.ts`) | The refusals of a code: `WRONG_CODE` with `attemptsLeft` (1 to 4), `CODE_EXPIRED` (expired, used, or never sent), `TOO_MANY_ATTEMPTS` (void after 5 wrong tries, even for the right code).                                            |
| `signInRefusedSchema`, `SignInRefusedBody` (`sign-in.ts`)                      | Every `400` of signing in: those three or `VALIDATION_FAILED`.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `signOutEndpoint` (`sign-out.ts`)                                              | The endpoint: `200` signed out, `403` the session from another site, `500` the session could not be deleted (its cookie kept).                                                                                                        |
| `signedOutSchema`, `SignedOut` (`sign-out.ts`)                                 | The answer `{ signedOut: true }`.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `crossSiteSessionSchema`, `CrossSiteSessionBody` (`sign-out.ts`)               | `403 FORBIDDEN` to a change sent with the session from a page of another site, or without `Origin` (CSRF).                                                                                                                            |
| `sessionEndpoint` (`session.ts`)                                               | `GET /api/auth/session`, answered to anyone, as every page asks it: `200` the account of the browser's session or `null` without one (never `401`), `500` the session could not be read.                                              |
| `signedInAccountSchema`, `SignedInAccount` (`session.ts`)                      | Its answer `{ name, email }`: the account the browser is signed in as; `name` is empty until the account creates its first event or names itself.                                                                                     |

**Depends on:** `packages/contracts/src`.

**Used by:** `apps/api/src/http`, `apps/api/src/modules/auth`, `apps/web/src/app/core/auth`, `apps/web/src/app/core/layout`.

### `packages/contracts/src/date-interpretations`

**Summary:** The endpoint that reads a date phrase.

**Purpose:** `POST /api/date-interpretations` reads the dates and times of a phrase in plain language, for the page that creates an event to mark: nothing is stored, and every phrase gets an answer (what is not understood comes as notices, never as an error). Only qando's pages may send it, at most 20 phrases in any hour per address. The answer has the shape of what `readDates` of `packages/date-reader` answers, written here as schemas so that the contract depends on no other package; dates and times carry no zone.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                              | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `dateInterpretationEndpoint` (`date-interpretation.ts`)                             | The endpoint: `200` what the phrase says, `400` a broken body, `403` from a page of another site or without `Origin`, `429` too many phrases from the address, `500`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `dateInterpretationBodySchema`, `DateInterpretationBody` (`date-interpretation.ts`) | Its body `{ text, timeZone, region? }`, nothing else: the phrase (1 to 120 characters once trimmed), the IANA name of the time zone it is written in (one the runtime knows; today and the time now are taken in it) and the region of the browser's language (two capital letters: `ES`, `US`), which orders a date in figures; any other text is read as no region, never refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `dateInterpretationSchema`, `DateInterpretation` (`date-interpretation.ts`)         | Its answer `{ options, questions, notices }`, each in the phrase's order.
