| `firstOptionDay(options)` (`new-event.ts`)                                                                       | The earliest day of any option, in any order (a date, or the first day of a range); null without options. The day the warning of `closesAfterFirstOption` speaks of.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `NewEventInput`, `NewEvent`, `NewEventProblem`, `NewEventValidation` (`new-event.ts`)                            | The types of the check's input and answers.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `localDate(instant, timeZone)` (`local-date.ts`, not re-exported)                                                | The calendar date an instant falls on in a time zone.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `apps/api/src/email`, `apps/api/src/modules/events`, `apps/api/src/modules/management`, `apps/api/src/modules/participations`, `apps/api/src/modules/public-events`, `apps/api/src/modules/results`, `apps/api/src/seed`, `apps/api/src/test`, `apps/web/src/app/features/create`, `apps/web/src/app/features/vote`, `apps/web/src/app/shared/ui`, `packages/date-reader/src`.

### `tools`

**Summary:** Scripts that check the repository.

**Purpose:** Checks that run with `npm run check` and in CI but are part of no application; each is a workspace of its own, with its tests and mutation testing.

**Contents:** `guide-check/`.

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `.`

### `tools/guide-check`

**Summary:** Checks this guide against the code, and that no file points outside.

**Purpose:** Two checks, both part of `npm run check`. `npm run check:guide` lists, one line each (`✗ <folder>: …`), every folder without a section, every section of a folder that no longer exists, every export a folder's section does not name and every input, output or model a component's row does not name, and fails if there is any. It checks presence, not truth. `npm run check:references` lists, one line each (`<path>:<line>: …`), every identifier in a text file that points to a document kept outside this repository (the identifier of a bug, a decision or a requirement; a decision record named by its number), and fails if there is any: who reads the code here cannot open that document, so a comment says its reason in words. It reads every text file but `package-lock.json` and its own unit tests, which must write such identifiers to try them. For both checks the files are those git knows, so ignored output never counts.

**Contents:** `src/`; `stryker.config.mjs` (threshold 70 %, `main.ts` and `references-main.ts` not mutated).

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `.`, `.github/workflows`.

### `tools/guide-check/src`

**Summary:** The two checks and their commands.

**Purpose:** Each check is a pure function with a command that feeds it. `guide-check.ts` holds the whole guide check as a function over the guide's text and the repository's files; `main.ts` lists the files with git, reads them, prints the problems and sets the exit code. `foreign-references.ts` holds the search for identifiers that point outside the repository as a function over the text of the files; `references-main.ts` lists the files with git, leaves out the binary ones, `package-lock.json` and that check's own unit tests, prints what is found and sets the exit code.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                 | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `guideProblems(guide, paths, read)` (`guide-check.ts`) | Every place where the guide and the code disagree, one line each, in folder order; empty when the guide is up to date. `paths` are the repository's files; `read` is called only for the TypeScript sources the guide must describe (not tests or `.d.ts`). Generated folders need no section, wherever they are; sources are read with the TypeScript parser, so comments and strings never count.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `foreignReferences(files)` (`foreign-references.ts`)   | Every identifier in the files that points to a document outside the repository, one line each (`<path>:<line>: …` with the identifier and what to do instead), in the order of the files and of their text; empty when there is none. An identifier is a known upper-case prefix, a hyphen and two or three digits, or a decision record named by its four-digit number; names that only look alike (a hash or a standard with its number, an issue number, the same letters inside a longer word or in lower case) are left alone. It has no exceptions: which files are read is the command's choice. |
| `TextFile` (`foreign-references.ts`)                   | A text file as `foreignReferences` takes it: its `path` from the repository's root and its whole `text`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `tools/guide-check`.

### `e2e`

**Summary:** Acceptance tests (Playwright + Gherkin).

**Purpose:** The user stories as executable scenarios, run in Chromium with playwright-bdd against the real web, API and database. A feature runs once its story is being built (the list in `playwright.config.ts`); scenarios not built yet carry `@wip`, and those waiting on a later milestone `@m2` or `@m3` (with a comment naming what they wait for); both run as expected to fail, so one that starts passing turns the run red until its tag is removed. A scenario tagged `@system-clock` waits for a time no scenario can move (a sign-in code's expiry, which the sign-in library reads from the system's clock) and is skipped: the API's integration tests check it. A scenario tagged `@touch` runs in a browser with a touch screen and no mouse (its pages match `(hover: none), (pointer: coarse)`), where the steps press, rest and slide a real finger.

**Contents:** `features/`, `steps/`, `mailbox/`, `layout/`; `settled.ts` (`settled(page)`: waits for the page's finite animations, so what is checked is read at rest); `accessibility.ts` (`expectAccessible(page)`: checks the page with axe against WCAG 2.2 AA once at rest, failing with each rule broken and its elements; a page whose markup has not changed since its last check is skipped); `api-process.ts` (how a run starts the API: `apiCommand` and `apiEnvironment`, the test clock on and its emails sent to the mailbox; `RestartedApi`, the API after a restart for one scenario: a second process on the same database, which answers every API request of the pages sent to it with `serve(page, headers)` and keeps each answer it gave (`answered`), while the run's own API keeps serving the other scenarios), `playwright.config.ts` (starts the mailbox, the API as `api-process.ts` says, and the web, or targets `E2E_BASE_URL`; runs the three screens, or the one `E2E_SCREEN` names, as each of CI's jobs does; every test at 375, 768 and 1280 px: the features in the `phone`, `tablet` and `desktop` projects, `layout/` in `layout <width>`), `global-setup.ts` (applies the migrations to `DATABASE_URL` before any scenario; `databaseUrl()` reads it, or says how to set it).

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/seed`, `apps/web`, `apps/api`.

**Used by:** `.github/workflows`.

### `e2e/features`

**Summary:** One Gherkin feature per user story.

**Purpose:** The acceptance criteria of each story (HU01–HU10) written as scenarios, plus a smoke test that the web, the API and the database work together.

**Contents:** `hu01-sign-in-with-code.feature` … `hu10-plain-language-dates.feature`, `smoke.feature`. Running today: `smoke.feature`, `hu01-sign-in-with-code.feature`, `hu03-create-event.feature`, `hu06-vote.feature`, `hu07-results.feature`, `hu08-confirm-date.feature`, `hu10-plain-language-dates.feature`.

**Depends on:** `e2e/steps`.

**Used by:** `e2e`.

### `e2e/steps`

**Summary:** Step definitions and fixtures.

**Purpose:** What each Gherkin step does in the browser or the database, and the per-scenario state the steps share.

**Contents:** `common.steps.ts` (opening pages and reading what they show), `events.steps.ts` (storing events, their votes and how they ended), `vote.steps.ts` (the voting page, and votes sent to the API directly), `results.steps.ts` (the results page, and results asked of the API directly), `manage.steps.ts` (the management page, and confirmations asked of the API directly), `create.steps.ts` (the creation wizard's steps up to its summary, and the service's health asked through the web), `create-finish.steps.ts` (the wizard's last step and "¡Hecho!": events created through the wizard, the options participants are served through the participation link, the management and results links opened without the organizer's session, the event after an API restart, the deadline and its warning, and a failure of the server), `phrase.steps.ts` (the smart calendar of the dates step: what is asked for, what it leaves on the calendar and tells, the button of the message that undoes it, its microphone with a recognizer that says what the scenario tells it to, and where the browser's requests went), `dates-calendar.ts` (the calendar of the dates step as a scenario finds it, for both), `sign-in.steps.ts` (the sign-in page: codes read from the mailbox and typed, wrong ones, the wait to ask for another and the limit of codes; and the management data asked of the API directly, without a credential or signed in as another account; signing in as an account with a name), `emails.steps.ts` (events created through the API and the emails they send, the outbox run at the scenario's time), `create-api.steps.ts` (events the API is asked to create directly, with broken options, and that none was created), `clock.steps.ts` (setting the scenario's time and moving it forward), `pending.hooks.ts` (the `@wip`, `@m2`, `@m3` handling, and the `@system-clock` scenarios skipped), `accessibility.hooks.ts` (after every step, `expectAccessible` on each page the scenario looks at; a step that opens and closes a dialog checks it while it is open), `fixtures.ts`.

**Exports:**

| Export                                                                                                                                          | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `test` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                          | Playwright's `test` extended with the fixtures below, for every step file; it gives a scenario tagged `@touch` a browser with a touch screen (`hasTouch`).                                                                                                                                        |
| `StoredEvents` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                  | The events a scenario stored, by title, so later steps open them by the name they read.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `ScenarioClock` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                 | The scenario's time, frozen in the browser and sent to the API in `X-Test-Now`; `advance(ms)` moves it forward for both.                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `OtherBrowsers` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                 | Fresh browsers besides the scenario's own, on the same time and address.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `MyVote` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                        | The vote the scenario cast through a page, for the steps that come back to it, and whether the page asked for a name.                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `ApiAnswer` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                     | The status the API gave to a request a step sent it directly (a vote no page would send, the results or a confirmation asked with the wrong link, an event created with broken options), and the body it answered, for the step that checks it.                                                   |
| `MyConfirmation` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                | The date the scenario confirmed through the API, for the step that checks it stayed confirmed.                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `Asked` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                         | What the scenario asked the smart calendar for: the phrase written last, and every request its browser made from the first page on (`requests`; `readings`, those that asked the API to read a phrase), for the steps that check where they went.                                                 |
| `MySignIn` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                      | The sign-in the scenario is in the middle of: the email its code went to, the wrong codes typed so far, and the private page it opened without a session (where signing in comes back to).                                                                                                        |
| `MySession` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                     | The session the scenario's browser signed in with, for the steps that come back to it: the account's email and the session's cookie as the browser sent it.                                                                                                                                       |
| `MyEvent` (`fixtures.ts`)                                                                                                                       | The event the scenario makes through the wizard, for the steps that come back to it: the title and the dates the wizard was given, and the options participants were served once it was created.                                                                                                  |
| `reachLastStep(wizard, title, rows)`, `OptionRow` (`create.steps.ts`)                                                                           | Walks the wizard as "Ana" from its first step to its last one, where "Crear" is, with the options a scenario writes (`OptionRow`: a date or a range of days, and the time it may have): the ranges of days saved, the dates marked, and each date's times through the times step and the summary. |
| `showMonthOf(page, date)` (`create.steps.ts`)                                                                                                   | Takes the calendar of the step on screen forward until it shows the month of a date.                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `openManagementLink(page, event)` (`manage.steps.ts`)                                                                                           | Opens the management link of a stored event, `/manage/:id#t=<token>`, as the organizer received it.                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `day(page, date)`, `calendar(page)`, `datesOnScreen(page)`, `monthArrow(page, name)`, `dayNames`, `rangeName(start, end)` (`dates-calendar.ts`) | The calendar of the wizard's dates step: a day of the month on screen by its date, the month on screen, the dates marked on it as screen readers name them (a range once), a month arrow, and how a day and a range are named.                                                                    |
| `press(target, hasTouch)` (`dates-calendar.ts`)                                                                                                 | A press with what the scenario's browser has: a finger on a touch screen, or the mouse.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `expectNoVoting(page)` (`vote.steps.ts`)                                                                                                        | Checks that nothing on the page lets the voter vote: no first-step choices, no days, no send.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `currentStep(page)` (`step-card.ts`)                                                                                                            | The step card on screen (the only region named "paso N"), so a step looks up its buttons and fields only there, never in the tabs above it or a step folding away.                                                                                                                                |
| `pageHeaders(baseURL, address, clock)` (`sign-in.steps.ts`)                                                                                     | The headers a request from qando's page carries (its `Origin`, the scenario's time), for the steps that ask the API directly as a page would.                                                                                                                                                     |
| `signInThroughTheApi(request, headers, mailbox, email)` (`sign-in.steps.ts`) | Signs a request context in as the address a step names, with the code emailed to it, for the steps that ask the API directly as a signed-in page would. |
| `localInstant(date, time, timeZone)` (`clock.steps.ts`)                                                                                         | The instant the clocks of a time zone show `time` on `date`; refuses times skipped or repeated by a clock change.                                                                                                                                                                                 |

**Depends on:** `apps/api/src/seed`, `e2e/mailbox`.

**Used by:** `e2e/features`.

### `e2e/mailbox`

**Summary:** Where the acceptance run's emails land.

**Purpose:** The API of the acceptance run sends every email to an SMTP server on this computer that keeps them in memory, so scenarios read the sign-in codes a person would find in their inbox, and nothing is sent to anyone. Each scenario has addresses of its own: codes, send limits and accounts go by email, and scenarios run at once, at three widths, on a database that earlier runs also used.

**Contents:** `server.ts` (the mailbox's server, started by `playwright.config.ts`: takes the API's emails over SMTP on port 2525, without TLS, and answers the ones delivered to an address as JSON, `GET /emails?to=<address>` on port 2526; `PUT /refused?to=<address>` makes it refuse that address with a temporary failure, as a server that is down would, until `DELETE`), `mailbox.ts`.

**Exports:**

| Export                          | What it does                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `Mailbox` (`mailbox.ts`)        | The emails of one scenario: `addressOf(named)` gives the scenario's own address for the one a step names (`ana@example.com`), the same through the scenario; `emailsTo(named)` the emails delivered to it so far, oldest first; `codesSentTo(named)` the codes of the sign-in emails among them (an event's emails go to the same address); `goDown()` and `comeBack()` make the mailbox refuse and take again the scenario's addresses so far (the sign-in comes before, as its code is emailed too); `read` the email a step read last, for the steps that say "it". |
| `DeliveredEmail` (`mailbox.ts`) | An email as the mailbox took it: who it was for, its subject and its plain text.                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `mailboxPorts` (`mailbox.ts`)   | The ports the mailbox listens on: `smtp` for the API, `http` for the steps.                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

**Depends on:** —

**Used by:** `e2e` (`playwright.config.ts`), `e2e/steps`.

### `e2e/layout`

**Summary:** Checks of the rendered layout.

**Purpose:** What only a browser can measure, which the unit tests (jsdom, no layout) cannot: the widths of the results bars, each answer its exact share of the 250 px bar while its count fits and one too narrow for its count grown to fit it, and a results page that never scrolls sideways; a management page that never scrolls sideways (its question neither), drops the token from the address bar and is used with the keyboard alone; a vote cast with the keyboard alone; a sign-in page that never scrolls sideways with a long email in either step, signs in with the keyboard alone and takes a pasted code with spaces, back on the page that asked for it; on the voting, results, management and sign-in pages, every Tab stop drawn differently while it has the focus; and the chips of dates that overlap, in their modal on a phone, focused and checked with axe. Plain Playwright tests that answer the API themselves, so they store nothing and run against any URL. On the wizard's times step: the "−" of a saved time and its shadow are whole wherever its pill is, the shadow of a column's well falls on its hours alone, and the hours of a column and its saved times rest on a whole one once a scroll is let go, also after a drag that scrolls the list.

**Contents:** `calendar-slide.spec.ts`, `manage-page.spec.ts`, `phrase-box.spec.ts` (the smart calendar's box is as large as the calendar it covers, and nothing under it moves when it opens and closes; its microphone is at the end of the text's well, half-way up, and the text ends before it, or goes to the well's end where the browser takes no dictation), `results-bars.spec.ts`, `sign-in-page.spec.ts`, `text-fields.spec.ts`, `times-saved.spec.ts`, `times-scroll.spec.ts`, `times-trash.spec.ts`, `vote-page.spec.ts`; `times-step.ts` (`openTimes(page)`: goes through the wizard to its times step, with a column for each of three days; `columns(page)`: the columns of that step); `focus.ts` (`expectEveryStopShowsFocus(page)`: walks the page with Tab and checks each stop, with its label, looks different focused and blurred; `tabTo(page, target)`: presses Tab until `target` has the focus).

**Depends on:** `apps/web`.

**Used by:** `e2e`.
